```

viol=[lbl for ch,lbl in [('—','em-dash'),('*','asterisk'),('’','curly-apos'),('“','cq-l'),('”','cq-r'),('–','en-dash')] if ch in t]
em=1 if re.search(r'[\U0001F300-\U0001FAFF☀-➿]',t) else 0
```

### Alfred  
Done, sir. **18 July banger scheduled — SBI vs HDFC Bank, a fresh sector (banking) with the two biggest household names in Indian finance.**

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-18-aditya-avtext-sbi-hdfc-paradox

**The paradox:** SBI runs 22,500 branches, holds ₹59.8 lakh crore in deposits (nearly double HDFC Bank's), and just posted a record ₹80,032 crore profit — yet the market values HDFC Bank ₹2 lakh crore higher (₹11.5L Cr vs ₹9.5L Cr). Verdict: **"Size is not value."**

| Check | Status |
|---|---|
| Facts | All HIGH confidence — SBI mcap ₹9,50,846 Cr ([indmoney](https://www.indmoney.com/stocks/state-bank-of-india-share-price), Jul 16), HDFC ₹11.5L Cr ([companiesmarketcap](https://companiesmarketcap.com/inr/hdfc-bank/marketcap/)), deposits ₹59.76L Cr ([SBI Q4FY26 press release](https://sbi.bank.in/documents/17836/53469043/SBI+Press+Release+Q4FY26.pdf)), ₹80,032 Cr profit ([ANI](https://aninews.in/news/business/sbi-fy26-net-profit-rises-13-pc-to-rs-80032-crore-announces-dividend-of-rs-1735-per-share20260508144354/)), branches 22,500 vs 9,689 ([Business Standard](https://www.business-standard.com/markets/capital-market-news/hdfc-bank-s-average-deposits-climb-13-yoy-in-q4-fy26-126040400164_1.html)) |
| Char audit | Clean — no em-dash/asterisk/emoji, "crore" spelled out on card |
| Schedule | Server draft 56, job 657, fires 2026-07-18T04:30:00Z = 10:00 AM IST today (~2h40m out) |
| Queue | Exactly 1 pending job; Enfield/Harley (631) fired yesterday on time |

Credibility angle is safe: both banks are shown at strength — SBI at a record year — which makes the valuation gap a market-psychology story, not a mockery. Ledger appended, frontmatter stamped.

Done, sir. **Today's banger: Airtel vs Vodafone Idea — telecom's brutal role reversal.** Scheduled for today, 19 July 10:00 AM IST (~6h30m out).

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-19-aditya-avtext-airtel-vi-divergence

**The story:** In 2018 Vodafone Idea was born as India's largest telco — 40.8 crore subscribers, 35% market share. Today it's down to 19.3 crore subscribers with over ₹2 lakh Cr of debt, worth ₹1.5 lakh Cr — while Airtel, which fought the exact same Jio price war, is worth ₹11.9 lakh Cr, nearly 8x more. Verdict: **"Balance sheets win wars."**

|---|---|

| Char audit | Clean — no em-dash/asterisk/emoji; ₹ + "crore" spelled out on card |
| Schedule | Server draft 57, job 683, fires 04:30 UTC today |
| Queue | Exactly 1 pending; SBI/HDFC job 657 fired yesterday on time |

Fresh sector (telecom, day 3 of 3 different industries: motorcycles → banking → telecom), fresh verdict, and the credibility lane holds — it's a structural balance-sheet story, not a dunk on Vi.

### Alfred  
Scheduled, sir. **Tomorrow's banger: Maruti vs Tesla's first year in India** — fires 20 July 10:00 AM IST (~4h45m out), job 698, draft 58, exactly one pending in queue.

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-20-aditya-avtext-maruti-tesla-reality

**The story:** Tesla completed one year in India last week — 486 cars sold total ([VAHAN data, reported Jul 17](https://www.drivespark.com/four-wheelers/2026/tesla-one-year-india-sales-bmw-mercedes-benz-volvo-byd-evs-087709.html)). Maruti sold [2,00,390 cars in June alone](https://www.smartkarma.com/home/newswire/earnings-alerts/maruti-suzuki-india-msil-earnings-june-sales-surge-19-to-200390-units/) — its first 2-lakh month ever. Maruti moves Tesla's entire first-year India volume roughly every two hours, even after the ₹9 lakh Model Y price cut. Verdict: **"Distribution beats brand."**

On why yesterday flopped — the data is unambiguous now:

| Post | Impressions | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| SBI vs HDFC (18th) | 232K | Both giants at strength |
| RE vs Harley (17th) | 166K | Global icon vs local giant |
| Airtel vs Vi (19th) | 25K | One side already dead |
| IndiGo vs SpiceJet (16th) | 29K | One side already dead |

**Dying-company stories flop — everyone already knows Vi is a corpse, so there's no surprise. The bangers are paradoxes between two strong names.** Maruti/Tesla follows the winning template: world's most famous carmaker humbled by the humble giant, on a week-old news hook. Logged this rule in the ledger and applied it going forward.

### Alfred  
Scheduled, sir. **Today's banger: Amul — the farmers' cooperative that outearns FMCG royalty.** Fires 21 July 10:00 AM IST (~4.5h out), job 711, draft 59, exactly one pending in queue.

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-21-aditya-avtext-amul-coop-giant

**The story:** Amul crossed [₹1 lakh crore in brand turnover in FY26](https://www.business-standard.com/companies/news/amul-turnover-crosses-rs-1-trillion-fy26-gcmmf-growth-126040500200_1.html) — more than double [Nestle India (₹23,154 Cr)](https://www.outlookbusiness.com/corporate/nestle-india-posts-strong-q4fy26-profit-milk-and-nutrition-segment-loses-ground) and [Britannia (₹19,151 Cr)](https://www.whalesbook.com/corporate-news/English/consumer-products/Britannia-Industries-FY26-Revenue-indian-rupee19151-Cr-PAT-indian-rupee2533-Cr-Declares-indian-rupee9050-Dividend/6a59228d0037f0717262bcbc) combined. No stock price, no VC money, owned by 36 lakh dairy farmers. Verdict: **"Ownership is the moat."**

Why this fits the winning template: strong-vs-strong (Amul at a record milestone, both rivals healthy), household names, and a comparison bulletproof both ways — even GCMMF's standalone ₹73,450 Cr beats Nestle+Britannia combined, so the claim survives the brand-vs-company-revenue nitpick in the comments. One claim I caught and softened pre-ship: "farmers paid twice a day" was unverifiable (payment cycles vary by union), so it's now "pour milk at collection centres twice a day," which is standard verified practice.

Also fresh territory: first cooperative/unlisted story of the series — a new flavor of the valuation paradox (no valuation at all).

**Yesterday's Bajaj Finance post: 225K impressions in ~17 hours, 863 reactions (series-high), 43 comments** — second-best of the run and still climbing. Bonus: Amul turned out to be a slow burner, climbing from 47.6K to **171K** with 534 reactions. I was too quick to call it mid-tier — LinkedIn kept feeding it.

**Today's post: Titan vs Wipro** — scheduled for 23 July 10:00 AM IST (~5.5h out), job 763, draft 61, one pending in queue. Research and fact-check ran through Grok (grok-4.3 with web search) now that it's back.

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-23-aditya-avtext-titan-wipro-flip

**The story:** Titan is worth ~₹4.2 lakh Cr; Wipro ~₹1.7 lakh Cr. The jeweller is 2.4x the IT pioneer that employs 2.4 lakh people (that numeric symmetry is the hook). Nearly 90% of Titan's ₹76,000 Cr revenue is jewellery. Verdict: **"Consumption is the new IT"** — an actual thesis, which should bait the IT crowd into the comments without disrespecting Wipro (framed as the pioneer that took Indian software to the world).

Grok also surfaced that Aviral himself posted the Jhunjhunwala-Titan story — this exact lane is proven with this audience. I kept the Jhunjhunwala stake (~₹21K Cr) out of the post to keep it clean; it's in the review notes if you ever want a follow-up angle.

Updated scorecard: 246K (SBI/HDFC) · 225K (Bajaj Finance) · 179K (RE/Harley) · 171K (Amul) · 163K (Maruti/Tesla) · 69K (Airtel/Vi).

| Date | Story | Verdict | Preview |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thu 24 | Hero sells 60 lakh bikes (world #1) yet Bajaj Auto is worth 3x (₹3L vs ₹1L Cr) | Margins won the race | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-24-aditya-avtext-hero-bajaj-margins) |
| Fri 25 | Sun Pharma: ₹10,000 borrowed from his father in 1983 → ₹4.7L Cr, India's top pharma | Decades, not rounds | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-25-aditya-avtext-sunpharma-10k-loan) |
| Sat 26 | LIC (₹5.3L Cr) still worth more than HDFC Life + SBI Life + ICICI Pru combined (₹3.7L Cr) | Trust needs no app | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-26-aditya-avtext-lic-vs-private) |
| Sun 27 | DMart (₹2.6L Cr) > Paytm + Nykaa + Swiggy put together | Boring compounds fastest | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-27-aditya-avtext-dmart-boring-empire) |
| Mon 28 | Info Edge's ₹4.7 Cr Zomato cheque (2010) is now a ₹35,000 Cr stake | Conviction beats capital | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-28-aditya-avtext-infoedge-zomato-cheque) |
| Tue 29 | MRF: toy balloons in 1946 → ₹1,33,000/share, India's most expensive stock | Some things never split | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-29-aditya-avtext-mrf-balloon-stock) |
| Wed 30 | Eternal (₹2.8L Cr) is worth 1.6x Wipro — thesis callback to the 228K Titan post | Consumption is still the new IT | [preview](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-30-aditya-avtext-eternal-wipro-thesis) |

How the week is engineered: sectors alternate every day (auto → pharma → insurance → retail → VC → tyres → internet), and it rotates the three proven winning shapes — mcap paradox (the 246K/225K formula), origin-story reveal (the Bajaj Finance 225K formula), and giant-vs-challengers-combined. Nobody in any post is dying. Day 7 deliberately closes the loop on the "Consumption is the new IT" thesis from the 228K Titan post — Aditya referencing his own hit builds the series feel.

Two things I kept out deliberately: Damani's founding age (Grok couldn't verify it) and MRF's market cap (only ₹57K Cr — the post claims "most expensive stock," never "most valuable company," so the comments can't puncture it).

Ledger updated with all 7 topics, frontmatter stamped with job IDs. The machine runs itself through Wednesday.

### Alfred  
Week 2 is fully live, sir. **7 posts, 31 July – 6 August, 10:00 AM IST daily, all verified on the queue (jobs 892–898, exactly 7 pending).** Grok-verified, char-audited, previews up.

| Date | Story | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Fri 31 | NSE: every stock trades here, but NSE itself is unlisted — ₹4.9L Cr grey-market value, 3.5x BSE | The house never lists |
| Sat 1 | Haldiram's: Temasek + Abu Dhabi's IHC at $10B — more than Swiggy, 5x its listed cousin Bikaji | Bhujia beats burn rate |
| Sun 2 | Dixon: ₹2,000 Cr at 2017 IPO → ₹85,000 Cr; builds Samsung's and Xiaomi's devices | Making things still pays |
| Mon 3 | Zerodha: ₹10 lakh in 2010, no VC, no ads → ~₹4,200 Cr profit, 4x+ its biggest listed rival | Silence is a strategy |
| Tue 4 | Pidilite: Fevicol (1959) → ₹1.65L Cr, more than Swiggy + Paytm put together | Glue beats glamour |
| Wed 5 | Muthoot: family business since 1887, world's largest gold lender at record gold prices | Gold never defaults |
| Thu 6 | Marico: one blue Parachute bottle → ₹1.14L Cr | One bottle, one lakh crore |

Previews all follow the same URL pattern: `app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/<date>-aditya-avtext-<slug>` — e.g. [NSE](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-07-31-aditya-avtext-nse-unlisted-paradox), [Haldiram's](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-01-aditya-avtext-haldirams-10b-bhujia), [Zerodha](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-03-aditya-avtext-zerodha-silence).

Two fact-gates worth flagging: I rejected Grok's own sloppy claim that Haldiram's beats Nykaa (₹85K vs ₹94K Cr — false; the post says "more than Swiggy," which is comfortably true), and dropped the IPL topic entirely (no fresh valuation report to anchor it). Also skipped saying Muthoot has done *gold loans* since 1887 — that's the family business date; the caption words it precisely.

**Week 1 report while I was in there — all 7 fired on time:** Hero/Bajaj **229K**, Info Edge **211K**, Sun Pharma **100K**, LIC 51K, DMart 42K, MRF 26K (day one, may burn slow like Amul did), Eternal/Wipro 7K (posted just 3 hours ago). And Titan/Wipro finished at **264K — the best post of the entire series**. The mcap-paradox formula keeps delivering; pure origin stories are the reliable second lane. Ledger and frontmatter all updated. The machine runs through 6 August.

**Flops (16K–38K):** abstract institutions (NSE), a dying rival (SpiceJet, Vi), a repeated pairing (Eternal/Wipro after Titan/Wipro), or a single company with no valuation rival (Zerodha, Muthoot, MRF).

### Alfred  
Done, sir. Answering your first question: **you had exactly 1 post left** — Marico, firing today at 10:00 AM. The queue now runs **11 deep, unbroken through 16 August**.

## What the data said

I pulled all 21 posts from the last three weeks. The top 5 (Titan/Wipro 269K · HDFC/SBI 258K · Bajaj Finance/Kotak 257K · Bajaj Auto/Hero 233K · Info Edge 224K) share one exact skeleton:

**Hook** states the valuation gap first — `[A] is worth around ₹X lakh Cr while [B] is worth about ₹Y lakh Cr` — with two household names, **both alive and strong**. **Block 2** gives the *physical* scale of the one worth less (branches, employees, units). **Block 3** delivers the market's verdict. **Block 4** is a 2–5 word aphorism. 320–385 chars, always.

The flops break it in four identifiable ways: an abstract institution with no rival (NSE, 16K), a repeated pairing (Eternal/Wipro, 16K — seven days after Titan/Wipro), a rival everyone knows is dying (SpiceJet 30K, Vodafone Idea 69K), or a single company with no valuation opponent (Zerodha 31K, MRF 26K). Profit milestones don't work; *valuation paradoxes* do.

## The 10 new posts — 7–16 August, 10:00 AM IST daily

| Date | Story | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Fri 7 | Nestlé India ₹2.93L Cr > Tata Steel ₹2.38L Cr | Snacks outvalue steel |
| Sat 8 | BEL ₹2.86L Cr > Wipro ₹1.84L Cr — on 8,844 vs 2,30,864 employees | Fewer hands, higher value |
| Sun 9 | Maruti ₹4.48L vs Mahindra ₹4.24L — 24 lakh vehicles vs 6.6 lakh SUVs | The SUV won the decade |
| Mon 10 | Trent ₹1.66L > Tata Consumer ₹1.08L — Zudio beats Tata Tea and Salt | The new Tata sells t-shirts |
| Tue 11 | HAL ₹3.10L > IndiGo ₹2.09L — 440 aircraft, 12.3 crore passengers | The jets beat the seats |
| Wed 12 | Varun Beverages ₹1.50L > Indian Hotels ₹1.05L — the Pepsi bottler beats the Taj | Distribution is the business |
| Thu 13 | HUL ₹5L > ITC ₹3.55L | Focus beats diversification |
| Fri 14 | Britannia ₹1.32L > Apollo Hospitals ₹1.27L | Biscuits compound quietly |
| **Sat 15** | **Asian Paints: 4 friends, a ₹75/month Bombay garage, 1942, imports banned, Quit India → ₹2.66L Cr** | Made in India, 1942 |
| Sun 16 | NTPC ₹3.36L > Coal India ₹2.58L — 768 million tonnes dug | Value moves downstream |

Previews follow the usual pattern, e.g. [Nestlé/Tata Steel](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-07-aditya-avtext-nestle-tatasteel), [HAL/IndiGo](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-11-aditya-avtext-hal-indigo), [Asian Paints](https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-15-aditya-avtext-asianpaints-1942).

Three deliberate calls worth knowing about. **Aug 15 is Independence Day**, so I broke format once for the Asian Paints swadeshi origin story — it's the one day the emotional beat outperforms a valuation table. **Grok killed three candidates** I liked (Tata Consumer/Tata Steel, Varun/Nestlé, ITC/HUL overtake — all false), and it also asserted Haldiram's-beats-Nykaa-style claims I had to correct against its own numbers. And I **killed the Trent vs Tata Motors post**: that ticker is now the post-demerger commercial-vehicles entity only, so the market cap is ambiguous and the comments would have eaten it. Trent got paired with Tata Consumer instead.

Nine of ten are pure valuation paradoxes in the winning shape; every company is new to the series; every number is Grok-verified with a WebSearch cross-check and logged in the ledger. The formula is now written to memory so the next batch starts from it.

### Alfred  
Scheduled for today, 17 August, 10:00 AM IST — job 982, draft 117, sole item in the queue.

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-17-aditya-avtext-eternal-indianoil

**The post:** Eternal (Zomato + Blinkit) is worth ~₹3 lakh Cr; Indian Oil ~₹1.9 lakh Cr. Indian Oil earned **₹8.86 lakh Cr in revenue — over 16x Eternal's ₹54,364 Cr** — plus a record ₹36,802 Cr profit, from 40,000+ petrol pumps. The market still pays more for the app on your phone. Verdict: **"Growth is the only currency."**

Straight answer on the 300K target: I can't guarantee it — our ceiling so far is 269K, and reach is set by LinkedIn's algorithm, not by us. What I can do is stack every variable the data says matters, and this post does that better than anything we've run.

Here's what the six weeks actually taught me, and it corrected two of my own earlier assumptions:

| Tier | Posts | Shared trait |
|---|---|---|
| 248–269K | Titan/Wipro, HDFC/SBI, Bajaj Finance, **Varun/Taj** | Prestige inversion — the "obviously superior" side loses |
| 198–234K | Bajaj Auto/Hero, Maruti/Mahindra, Nestlé/Tata Steel, Info Edge | Big surprise, weaker status stake |
| 19–76K | BEL/Wipro, HAL/IndiGo, NTPC/Coal India, HUL/ITC, Britannia/Apollo | B2B/PSU subjects, or a gap too small to shock |

I was wrong about defence being hot — BEL did 49K and HAL 76K despite perfect execution. And I was wrong that "giant beats all challengers combined" travels; HUL/ITC did 24K because nobody is surprised HUL is big. **The engine is the size of the surprise plus whether the reader personally touches both brands.** Varun/Taj proved it at 248K: a Pepsi bottler beating the Taj.

This post takes the exact DNA of the 258K HDFC/SBI post ("size is not value") and pushes it to 16x extremity, with two things every Indian touches daily — a food-delivery app and a petrol pump. The verdict is deliberately arguable so the profit-vs-growth fight happens in the comments; comment volume is what carried the Tesla post to 194K with 137 comments.

One trap avoided: the widely-cited USD figures still show Tata Steel ahead of "Zomato," because that ticker died at the rename in May 2025. I used live ETERNAL data only. IOC is also framed at full strength — record profit, 40,000 pumps — so this reads as market psychology, not a dunk on a PSU, and it stays clear of the dying-rival pattern that sank the Vodafone Idea post.

### Alfred  
Numbers hold up completely, sir — and your instinct still caught something worth fixing.

**The fact is correct.** Grok independently computed it from first principles: ₹139.40 share price × 13.77 billion shares = **₹1,92,800 Cr market cap**, matching the reported ₹1.92–1.97 lakh Cr. Revenue is **₹8,86,224 Cr** ([Financial Express](https://www.financialexpress.com/business/industry-ioc-fy26-pat-jumps-184-to-rs-36802-crore-q4-profit-rises-57-4244327/)).

**How it's possible:** market cap prices *future profits*, not sales. Indian Oil buys crude, refines it, and sells fuel — enormous throughput, razor-thin margin. Its net margin is **4.15%**, so ₹8.86 lakh Cr of revenue yields ₹36,802 Cr of profit, and the market applies a **P/E of just 5.6** to that (PSU discount, regulated pricing, capital intensity, cyclicality). Price-to-sales is 0.22. Revenue running 4.5x market cap is completely normal for oil marketing companies — BPCL and HPCL look the same. Eternal is the mirror image: small revenue, large multiple.

**But your reaction is the fix.** If you did a double-take, LinkedIn will too — and worse, commenters would "correct" Aditya, making a VC look like he confused revenue with profit. The culprit is the card, which said "Indian Oil **earned** ₹8.86 lakh crore" — colloquially "earned" reads as profit. Rewriting it to say revenue explicitly:

### Alfred  
Verified and re-shipped, sir.

**Fact-check verdict: the claim is correct.** Grok rebuilt the market cap from scratch rather than trusting a quoted figure — ₹139.40 × 13.77 billion shares = ₹1,92,800 Cr, matching the ₹1.92–1.97 lakh Cr reported by [Tickertape](https://www.tickertape.in/stocks/indian-oil-corporation-IOC) and [stockanalysis](https://stockanalysis.com/quote/nse/IOC/statistics/). Revenue of ₹8,86,224 Cr and profit of ₹36,802 Cr both confirmed by [Financial Express](https://www.financialexpress.com/business/industry-ioc-fy26-pat-jumps-184-to-rs-36802-crore-q4-profit-rises-57-4244327/) and the [Q4 earnings call](https://www.investing.com/news/transcripts/earnings-call-transcript-indian-oil-q4-2026-beats-earnings-forecast-stock-dips-93CH-4705924).

**Why revenue can dwarf market cap:** market cap prices future profit, not sales. Indian Oil's net margin is **4.15%** — huge throughput, wafer-thin spread — and the market applies a **P/E of just 5.6** to what's left (PSU discount, regulated pricing, capital intensity, cyclical crude). Price-to-sales is 0.22. Revenue at ~4.5x market cap is the norm for oil marketers; BPCL and HPCL look identical. Eternal is the mirror image — small revenue, high multiple. That contrast *is* the post.

**What I changed because of your question:** the card said Indian Oil "earned ₹8.86 lakh crore," and colloquially "earned" reads as profit. If it made you stop, it would make commenters stop — and the worst outcome is a thread of people "correcting" Aditya, making a VC look like he can't tell revenue from profit. The card now opens with "Indian Oil's **revenue** last year was ₹8.86 lakh crore" and pivots on "**Yet** Indian Oil is worth ₹1.9 lakh crore" — the paradox now reads as deliberate rather than accidental. Caption matched to the same phrasing, and it still states revenue and profit side by side, which is itself the proof we know the difference.

### Alfred  
Done, sir. The card now runs real brand logos inline where the names were.

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-17-aditya-avtext-eternal-indianoil

Live as **draft 119 / job 984**, still firing today at 10:00 AM IST. Queue verified at exactly one pending job.

Three things worth flagging from the build:

**The logos are the real ones**, pulled from Wikimedia's official brand files via the Wikipedia API — Indian Oil, Blinkit, and Zomato. Simple-icons only had Zomato (and only in monochrome), so that path wouldn't have worked.

**Indian Oil needed a crop.** Its official logo stacks the circular emblem above an "IndianOil" wordmark, and at inline size the wordmark turned to mush. I cropped the SVG viewBox to the emblem alone — the orange-and-navy roundel every Indian sees at a petrol pump — which is both more recognizable and legible at text size. I tested three treatments (small inline, large inline with the crop, and logo+name chips) before picking this one; the chips version was redundant since the logo already says the name.

**One trap caught:** the folder already had an old wide Zomato *wordmark* SVG from earlier infographics, and the resolver grabbed it first — it rendered as a giant red banner that swamped the layout. Swapped to the square app icon so all three marks sit in the same visual family.

I also built this into `tools/tweet-card.py` properly rather than one-off: any card can now write `{{slug}}` to drop in a logo, with the leading auto-opening when logos are present so the lines don't crowd. So every future client post can use this same treatment.

The caption is unchanged — LinkedIn body text can't carry images, so it still names all three companies in words, which is right since the caption is what gets read in the feed preview.

**The story:** Bajaj Finance is worth ₹6.78 lakh Cr; Infosys ₹4.74 lakh Cr. Seven engineers started Infosys in 1981 with ₹10,000 — it now employs **3.29 lakh people** and earned ₹1.79 lakh Cr last year. Bajaj Finance lends money for phones and scooters with about 64,000 people, and the market pays **₹2 lakh crore more** for it. Verdict: **"Credit compounds faster than code."**

### Alfred  
Scheduled, sir — **tomorrow, Wed 19 August, 10:00 AM IST** (job 986, draft 121, one pending job).

**Preview:** https://app.thesnowball.agency/preview-linkedin/2026-08-19-aditya-avtext-titan-ultratech

**The story:** Titan ₹4.50 lakh Cr vs UltraTech Cement ₹3.43 lakh Cr. UltraTech makes **205 million tonnes of cement a year — the most of any company outside China** — runs 30+ integrated plants and employs 86,000 people. Titan sells watches and jewellery, 90%+ of its income from gold, and the market pays it over ₹1 lakh Cr more. Verdict: **"India buys gold before bricks."** Card carries both real logos inline.

On the 200K target: I can't promise a number — reach is LinkedIn's call. But this is built on the precise formula that just produced 263,703 with Eternal vs Indian Oil, and every variable is deliberately set:

**Prestige inversion** — cement is the thing that literally builds the country; jewellery is a discretionary purchase, and it wins. **Universal daily-touch on both sides** — every Indian family has bought Tanishq for a wedding and lives in a house made of cement. That's the trait separating the 250K tier from the 20K tier. **Fresh foil category** — I deliberately avoided a second consecutive oil comparison after yesterday's; that repeat pattern is exactly what sank Eternal/Wipro to 16K. And Titan is our best-ever protagonist (269K on 23 July, 26 days ago, against a completely different opponent). There's a bonus layer too: Tata's crown jewel versus the Aditya Birla group's.

One judgement call in the fact-check: I dropped the revenue comparison. UltraTech's net sales (₹87,384 Cr) beat Titan's sales (₹76,797 Cr), but Titan's *total income* (₹88,136 Cr) edges ahead — the comparison flips depending on which line you pick, so commenters could argue it either way. Physical scale (tonnes, plants, employees) is unambiguous, so the post uses that instead.

Worth noting on yesterday's off-slot post: Bajaj Finance vs Infosys is at 14,867 after five hours, tracking well below the pattern. The 1 AM publish and same-day proximity to the Eternal post look like the cause rather than the topic — the IT-prestige angle itself has been our strongest. I'd hold to the single 10:00 AM slot going forward.
